Definition and Acronyms

[31] table
Acronym / Term | Definition
WMSD | Warehouse Management System
SRS | Software Requirements Specification
UC | Use Case
FE | Feature
BF | Business Flow
PO | Purchase Order
UoM | Unit of Measure
GR | Goods Receipt
Lot | A tile batch identified by number, tone and actual calibre
Usable stock | Accepted quantity eligible for goods issue or applicable movement
Physical stock | Quantities physically recorded across quality classifications
Cut-off snapshot | Stock balance preserved at the defined stocktake cut-off
N–X–T | Inbound–Outbound–Closing Stock report (local stock ledger terminology)

[34] Heading1
I. Overall Requirements

[35] Heading2
1. Context Diagram

[36] 
The WMSD supports internal tile-warehouse operations: access control, products and lots, suppliers, replenishment, purchase orders, receiving and inspection, storage locations, quality-specific balances, stock movements, stocktake, goods issue, returns, reports and lot traceability. External suppliers and customers are represented by source documents, not by assumed portal-user accounts.

[37] 
 

[38] 
Figure 1. System context diagram — source-defined interacting roles

[39] Heading2
2. Main Business Flow

[40] Heading3
2.1 Replenishment, Procurement and Goods Receiving (BF-01)

[41] 
Trigger: Inventory Staff identifies a replenishment need based on current stock levels and creates a replenishment request for review and approval.

[42] 
End condition: The replenishment request is rejected without further procurement, or the approved request results in a purchase order and completed goods receipt. Accepted quantities are recorded in inventory by lot, location and quality status, while outstanding purchase quantities are updated.

[43] 
 

[44] 
Figure 1. Replenishment, Procurement and Goods Receiving — actor/system workflow.

[45] 
Decision handling: A rejected replenishment request cannot proceed to purchase order creation. During receiving, accepted goods are recorded as usable inventory, while defective or discrepant quantities are segregated and documented. Only accepted quantities are posted to usable inventory. Outstanding purchase quantities are updated based on actual receipt records.

[46] Heading3
2.2 Defective Goods Isolation and Disposition (BF-02)

[47] 
Trigger: Inventory Staff identifies defective, damaged or otherwise non-usable goods during warehouse operations and records the defect information.

[48] 
End condition: The defective quantity is isolated and its disposition is approved and processed, with inventory quantities, final stock status and movement history updated accordingly. Rejected disposition proposals remain subject to revision and further review.

[49] 
 

[50] 
Figure 2. Defective Goods Isolation and Disposition — actor/system workflow.

[51] 
Decision handling: Identified defective quantities are separated from usable inventory to prevent unauthorized issuing or movement. If a disposition proposal is rejected, the rejection is recorded and the proposal must be revised before further processing. Only approved dispositions may be posted, and the system records the resulting stock changes and movement history.

[52] Heading3
2.3 Warehouse Transfer and Discrepancy Resolution (BF-03)

[53] 
Trigger: Warehouse Staff creates a warehouse transfer request to move inventory from a source warehouse to a destination warehouse.

[54] 
End condition: The transfer is completed after the dispatched and received quantities are reconciled, and the destination inventory is updated. Transfers with unresolved quantity discrepancies remain open until an approved resolution is recorded and applied.

[55] 
 

[56] 
Figure 3. Warehouse Transfer and Discrepancy Resolution — actor/system workflow.

[57] 
Decision handling: Upon dispatch, the system reduces usable inventory at the source warehouse and records the transferred quantity as In Transit. After receiving, the system compares dispatched and received quantities. Matching quantities allow the transfer to be completed. If a discrepancy exists, the system records the difference and creates a resolution request. Approved resolutions update the relevant inventory balances before closure; rejected or unresolved requests keep the transfer open.

[58] Heading3
2.4 Stocktake and Inventory Adjustment (BF-04)

[59] 
Trigger: An authorized user creates a stocktake to verify physical inventory against recorded stock balances at a defined cut-off time.

[60] 
End condition: The stocktake is completed when no variance is identified, or an approved inventory adjustment is posted for verified discrepancies. Inventory balances and adjustment history reflect the approved results.

[61] 
 

[62] 
Figure 4. Stocktake and Inventory Adjustment — actor/system workflow.

[63] 
Decision handling: The system compares physical counts with the book balance captured at the stocktake cut-off. If no variance is detected, the stocktake is marked as completed without an inventory adjustment. If a variance exists, staff performs a recount, records the variance cause and submits an adjustment request. Rejected adjustments do not change inventory balances. Approved adjustments update the inventory and preserve before/after quantities in the adjustment history.

[64] Heading3
2.5 Warehouse Issue and Stock Deduction (BF-05)

[65] 
Trigger: An authorized user creates a goods issue request specifying the source reference, products and requested quantities for warehouse fulfillment.

[66] 
End condition: The actual issued quantities are confirmed, the corresponding inventory is deducted from the selected lots and storage locations, and the goods issue status and inventory movement history are updated.

[67] 
 

[68] 
Figure 5. Warehouse Issue and Stock Deduction — actor/system workflow.

[69] 
Decision handling: Before confirming a goods issue, the system validates usable inventory by lot and location. If sufficient eligible stock is available, the confirmed quantity is deducted and the issue is recorded. If stock is insufficient, the system displays the shortage and requires the warehouse operator to revise the lot selection or issued quantity. Inventory deduction must not proceed until the revised quantities pass validation.

[70] Heading3
2.6 Customer Return and Returned Goods Classification (BF-06)

[71] 
Trigger: An authorized user records a customer return against an originating transaction, specifying the returned products, lots, quantities and return reasons.

[72] 
End condition: Returned goods have been inspected and classified, and an approved disposition has been applied. Reusable quantities are returned to usable inventory, while non-reusable quantities remain isolated under the appropriate stock status.

[73] 
 

[74] 
Figure 6. Customer Return and Returned Goods Classification — actor/system workflow.

[75] 
Decision handling: Returned goods are initially kept outside usable inventory pending inspection and disposition approval. If the proposed disposition is rejected, the returned quantity remains isolated and no usable inventory is increased. If approved, reusable goods are transferred to usable inventory, while damaged or non-reusable goods remain isolated under the corresponding status. Inventory changes must follow the approved classification.

[76] Heading3
2.7 Supplier Return and Inventory Reconciliation (BF-07)

[77] 
Trigger: An authorized user creates a supplier return request for goods that must be returned to the supplier, referencing the original receipt, product, lot, storage location, quantity and return reason.

[78] 
End condition: An approved supplier return is dispatched and completed, with the corresponding stock quantity deducted and its movement history recorded. Rejected returns are documented, and the affected goods remain subject to reclassification and appropriate inventory status handling.

[79] 
 

[80] 
Figure 7. Supplier Return and Inventory Reconciliation — actor/system workflow.

[81] 
Decision handling: Goods identified for supplier return are isolated and excluded from usable inventory while awaiting approval. If the return is approved, the system records dispatch, deducts the corresponding supplier-return inventory and completes the return with traceable movement history. If rejected, the system records the rejection, requires reclassification of the isolated goods and updates their inventory status without posting a supplier-return dispatch.

[82] Heading2
3. Conceptual Data Model

[83] Heading3
3.1 Entity Relationship Diagram

[84] 
The following Crow’s-Foot conceptual diagram follows the 15 business entities and the attribute-box presentation in the supplied ERD reference. Relationship labels are business verbs. Multiplicities are conceptual and should be confirmed against the final physical data model; PK/FK columns are intentionally omitted.

[85] 
 

[87] 
Figure 9. Conceptual entity relationship diagram — entity attributes and labelled cardinalities

[88] Heading3
3.2 Entity Descriptions

[89] 
Entity: Warehouse

[90] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Logical or physical warehouse referenced by put-away, stock, transfer and reporting.
Key business attributes | Warehouse ID, Name, Status
Business identity | Warehouse ID distinguishes warehouse records.
Status / lifecycle | Active or inactive for new selection.
UC traceability | UC-41–46; UC-61–69

[92] 
Entity: Defective Goods Case

[93] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Evidence-based record of defective or quarantined goods and authorized disposition.
Key business attributes | Case ID, Lot / Location, Defective Quantity, Disposition Status
Business identity | Case ID identifies the defect incident and links lot/location.
Status / lifecycle | Under review, approved/rejected, handled; technical enum not specified.
UC traceability | UC-49–56

[95] 
Entity: Goods Issue

[96] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Physical goods issue created against a source issue request.
Key business attributes | Issue ID, Source Request, Actual Quantity, Status
Business identity | Issue ID references the related issue request.
Status / lifecycle | Prepared and actual issued quantities separately retained.
UC traceability | UC-82–89

[98] 
Entity: Location

[99] table
Field | Value
Purpose | An addressable storage place in a warehouse, including area, aisle and rack.
Key business attributes | Location ID, Code, Area, Rack, Status
Business identity | Location code identifies the place within the warehouse.
Status / lifecycle | Active/inactive; cannot deactivate while any quantity remains.
UC traceability | UC-41–46

[101] 
Entity: Inventory Balance

[102] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Quantity for a product lot at a warehouse location and quality state.
Key business attributes | Balance ID, Product / Lot, Location, Usable Quantity, Quality Status
Business identity | Unique logical balance scope is warehouse, location, product, lot and quality status.
Status / lifecycle | Accepted, pending inspection, defective, quarantined or in transit as appropriate.
UC traceability | UC-47–56

[104] 
Entity: Warehouse Transfer

[105] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Stock transfer documenting source dispatch, handover, receipt and discrepancies.
Key business attributes | Transfer ID, Source / Destination, Dispatched / Received, Status
Business identity | Transfer ID identifies the cross-warehouse transaction.
Status / lifecycle | Planned, in transit, received, discrepancy pending; exact codes to confirm.
UC traceability | UC-61–69

[107] 
Entity: Lot

[108] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Actual product batch distinguished by lot, tone and calibre.
Key business attributes | Lot ID, Lot Number, Tone, Actual Calibre, Production Date
Business identity | Identity uses the product and lot-characteristic combination.
Status / lifecycle | Lot record retains link to posted stock movements.
UC traceability | UC-14–17

[110] 
Entity: Goods Return

[111] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Customer-return or supplier-return process and its resulting inventory status.
Key business attributes | Return ID, Return Type, Quantity, Approval Status
Business identity | Return ID, type and related source document distinguish returns.
Status / lifecycle | Inspected, pending decision, approved, dispatched/posted as applicable.
UC traceability | UC-90–101

[113] 
Entity: Stocktake

[114] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Count period/scope with immutable system balance at its cutoff.
Key business attributes | Stocktake ID, Cut-off Time, Scope, Status
Business identity | Stocktake ID and cutoff distinguish individual inventory counts.
Status / lifecycle | Created, snapshot captured, counted and reconciled; codes to confirm.
UC traceability | UC-70–76

[116] 
Entity: Inventory Adjustment

[117] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Request and resulting stock delta for a documented stocktake variance.
Key business attributes | Adjustment ID, Variance, Cause, Approval Status
Business identity | Adjustment ID links the request to its stocktake and approval.
Status / lifecycle | Pending approval, approved/rejected, posted; one valid post only.
UC traceability | UC-77–81

[119] 
Entity: Product

[120] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Tile catalogue item and its packaging/unit conversion definition.
Key business attributes | Product ID, Code, Name, Unit Conversion Rule
Business identity | Product code identifies the item; conversions are product-specific.
Status / lifecycle | Active/inactive; historical quantities remain linked.
UC traceability | UC-08–13

[122] 
Entity: Goods Receipt

[123] table
Field | Value
Purpose | An individual supplier delivery linked to its purchase order.
Key business attributes | Receipt ID, Receipt / Order Reference, Accepted Quantity, Status
Business identity | Receipt ID identifies each delivery separately.
Status / lifecycle | Receiving, inspected and accepted portions are tracked independently.
UC traceability | UC-33–40

[125] 
Entity: Replenishment Request

[126] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Request for stock replenishment requiring Store Manager decision.
Key business attributes | Request ID, Requested Quantity, Approval Status
Business identity | Request ID differentiates submitted proposals.
Status / lifecycle | Pending decision, approved, or rejected; exact system status codes unconfirmed.
UC traceability | UC-23–26

[128] 
Entity: Purchase Order

[129] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Supplier order created from an approved replenishment request.
Key business attributes | Order ID, Order Number, Supplier, Status
Business identity | Order number and individual item lines reference the request.
Status / lifecycle | Tracks ordered, confirmed, received and outstanding quantities.
UC traceability | UC-27–32

[131] 
Entity: Supplier

[132] table
Field | Value
Purpose | Supplier reference used for purchasing and supplier returns.
Key business attributes | Supplier ID, Name, Status
Business identity | Supplier code distinguishes supplier records.
Status / lifecycle | Active/inactive; inactive suppliers cannot be selected for new orders.
UC traceability | UC-18–22

[134] Heading3
3.3 Data Business Rules

[135] table
Rule | Description | Applies To
DR-01 | Product and lot identifiers must distinguish tone/calibre to avoid merging physically different tiles. | UC-10,16,36,47
DR-02 | Box/piece/m² conversions are product-specific and must not retrospectively change posted historical quantities. | UC-13,36,74
DR-03 | A purchase order may have multiple receipts; editing/canceling an outstanding part cannot erase receipts already posted. | UC-30–35
DR-04 | Only accepted goods or subsequently authorized accepted disposition increase usable stock; other quality states remain separate. | UC-37–40,49–56
DR-05 | A location containing stock in any quality state cannot be deactivated; inactive locations are not available for new transactions. | UC-43–46
DR-06 | Movement between locations of the same warehouse does not change overall warehouse quantity. | UC-59–60
DR-07 | Cross-warehouse transfer records dispatch, handover and destination receipt separately; discrepancies require evidence and approval. | UC-63–69
DR-08 | Stocktake compares counted quantity with an immutable system snapshot; later transactions remain separately identifiable. | UC-72–76
DR-09 | Inventory adjustments are posted only for approved requests and cannot be posted twice. | UC-79–81
DR-10 | Goods issue deducts actual issued quantity from the specified eligible lot/location without making available stock negative. | UC-84–89
DR-11 | Customer returns cannot increase usable stock until inspection and the disposition decision are completed. | UC-92–95
DR-12 | Supplier return dispatch does not deduct the same goods twice when they were rejected at receipt. | UC-98–101
DR-13 | Every posted stock movement is traceable to source document, original and resulting quantities, user and time. | UC-107–109
DR-14 | Deactivation of a master record prevents selection for new operations but must not erase historical references. | UC-05,06,12,22

[137] Heading2
4. User Requirements

[138] Heading3
4.1 Actors

[139] table
# | Actor | Description
1 | Store Manager | Creates/maintains products and approves replenishment, defective goods, transfer differences, stocktake adjustments and returns. Views applicable reports.
2 | Accountant | Maintains suppliers, replenishment requests, purchase orders, supplier confirmations and the documented purchasing/receiving views.
3 | Warehouse Staff | Records receipts, inspections, put-away, quality states, stock movement, stocktakes, actual issues and returns; accesses lot traceability.
4 | Sales Staff | Views permitted product/stock data, records dispatch requests and customer returns in the source-defined scope.
5 | Administrator | Manages accounts, roles and access permissions; is not automatically assigned operational warehouse permissions.

UC-94 | Classify returned goods disposition | FE-11 — Customer and Supplier Return Stock Handling | Record accepted, conditionally accepted, defective, or awaiting supplier return before posting inventory.
UC-95 | Approve or reject customer return disposition | FE-11 — Customer and Supplier Return Stock Handling | Approve/reject restocking, quarantine, or loss based on inspected results.
UC-96 | View supplier return request list | FE-11 — Customer and Supplier Return Stock Handling | Search by supplier, lot, reason, and approval status.
UC-97 | View supplier return request details | FE-11 — Customer and Supplier Return Stock Handling | Display lot, location, quantity, source receipt, evidence, and approval decision.
UC-98 | Create supplier return request | FE-11 — Customer and Supplier Return Stock Handling | Record lot, location, quantity, reason, and original receipt document.
UC-99 | Quarantine goods pending supplier return | FE-11 — Customer and Supplier Return Stock Handling | Reserve the specified lot/location quantity outside available stock until shipment.
UC-100 | Approve or reject supplier return request | FE-11 — Customer and Supplier Return Stock Handling | Record decision and reason before authorizing the return shipment.
UC-101 | Record actual supplier return dispatch | FE-11 — Customer and Supplier Return Stock Handling | Record actual goods returned without double-deducting goods previously rejected at receipt.
UC-102 | View inventory movement summary report | FE-12 — Warehouse Reports | Show opening, inbound, outbound, adjustment, and closing balances for a selected period.
UC-103 | View stock by lot and location report | FE-12 — Warehouse Reports | Show quantities by warehouse, location, lot, tone, and quality status.
UC-104 | View purchase order and receipt report | FE-12 — Warehouse Reports | Compare ordered, confirmed, received, and outstanding quantities.
UC-105 | View warehouse transfer report | FE-12 — Warehouse Reports | Track dispatched, in-transit, received, and discrepancy quantities.
UC-106 | View stocktake and adjustment report | FE-12 — Warehouse Reports | Show counts, variance reasons, and recorded adjustments.
UC-107 | View stock movement log | FE-13 — Inventory Audit Trail | Filter movements by product, lot, location, transaction type, and time.
UC-108 | View stock movement details | FE-13 — Inventory Audit Trail | Display source document, before/after quantity, operator, time, and reason.
UC-109 | Trace a product lot through source documents | FE-13 — Inventory Audit Trail | Link a lot's receipts, movements, issues, returns, and inventory adjustments.

[175] Heading1
II. Use Case Specifications

[176] Heading2
1. Access and Role Management (FE-01)

[177] Heading3
1.1 UC-01: Sign in to the system

[178] table
Primary Actors | Store Manager, Accountant, Warehouse Staff, Sales Staff, Administrator | Secondary Actors | None specified by the UC source
Description | As an authorized Store Manager/Accountant/Warehouse Staff/Sales Staff/Administrator user, I want to sign in to the system so that access authorized functions according to the assigned account and role.
Related feature/screen: FE-01 — Access and Role Management / SC-01.
Preconditions | Access & identity: An account exists; authentication is initiated by the entered credentials.
Source records / workflow state: The account exists and has permission to use the system.
Required inputs (business minimum): Username; password.
Optional or source-undefined inputs: None.
Validation and uniqueness: Credentials match an active account; role rights checked.
Technical field specification: Apply the SQL Server field definitions, precision, field entry requirements and validations in Part III for the linked screen. These are technical design defaults; additional business rules require a separately approved change.
Normal Flow | 1. An authorized store manager/accountant/warehouse staff/sales staff/administrator user opens SC-01 and selects “Sign in to the system”.
2. The system checks permission, displays the relevant form and current source values.
3. Enter username and password on the Sign In screen
4. System verifies the credentials and checks account activation
5. System determines permissions assigned to the authenticated role
6. System starts the authorized user session and opens the permitted work area.
7. The system validates all mandatory input fields, cross-document quantities, actor authorization and referenced record state before accepting confirmation.
8. On success the system saves the specific business outcome and updates related documents or eligible stock only if this UC explicitly performs a stock-posting operation.
9. The system displays the resulting record/status and source references; inventory-affecting operations retain the before/after movement details.
Alternative Flows | A1 — At step 2, access/role check fails: show MSG-08 and reveal no protected record; no data are changed.
A2 — At step 3–6, a required input is absent: identify the specific missing field (MSG-02); retain entered information without committing.
A3 — At the validation step 7, a reference is inactive, duplicate, outside the current workflow stage or violates the business rule: explain the specific field/record; do not partially post.
A4 — Business-specific exception: Invalid login, inactive user or denied account returns no session.
A5 — At source-defined 3: Missing or incorrect credentials: reject sign-in with a non-disclosing error message.
A5 — At source-defined 3: The account is inactive or unauthorized: prevent access without changing business data.
Postconditions | Successful outcome: An authorized session is established with the assigned role only.
No inventory balance is changed unless the described UC expressly authorizes a stock movement.
Failure outcome: Missing inputs, a rejected action or a failed validation leave existing posted transactions and inventory unchanged.
Traceability: Existing source references, responsible actor and action time are retained where this UC creates or updates a business record.

[180] Heading3
1.2 UC-02: View account list

[181] table
Primary Actors | Administrator | Secondary Actors | None specified by the UC source
Description | As a Administrator, I want to view account list so that search and filter accounts by name, role, and active status.
Related feature/screen: FE-01 — Access and Role Management / SC-02.
Preconditions | Access: The actor is signed in and authorized to view Account list under UC-02.
Reference data: Account records and role descriptions.
Optional search/filter inputs: Name, role, active/inactive status. No filters are mandatory; the actor may run an unfiltered list/report within permission scope.
Display fields: Account identifier, staff name, role, status. Values are retrieved from saved records; any displayed quantity is reconciled from the corresponding business transactions.
Result scope: Access rights and existing document/quality states determine which records can be opened.
Normal Flow | 1. The actor navigates to SC-02 and selects “View account list”.
2. The system checks role authorization and presents the permitted scope of records.
3. The actor optionally enters filters: Name, role, active/inactive status.
4. The system validates referenced record identity, supplied filter values and date-range order where applicable.
5. The system retrieves matching records using their current persisted links and permissions.
6. The system displays Account identifier, staff name, role, status with the relevant linked source references and recorded statuses.
7. The actor may inspect a result or adjust filters; this read-only action does not create, update or post inventory.
Alternative Flows | A1 — Step 2: Actor lacks view permission; show MSG-08 and reveal no protected data.
A2 — Step 3/4: A selected record identifier does not exist or is outside authorized scope; show a non-disclosing not-found/permission result.
A3 — Step 3/5: No record matches the filters; show MSG-01 and an empty list rather than sample or fabricated rows.
A4 — Step 4: Filters conflict (for example, From Date after To Date) or have an invalid value; highlight the filter, leave the stored data unchanged.
A5 — Step 5/6: A linked document is unavailable or historical/inactive; preserve the original reference and present only data for which the actor has access.
Postconditions | Successful outcome: The actor has seen the Account list and its relevant Account identifier, staff name, role, status.
Persisted business data: No creation, status transition, inventory change or approval occurs from viewing or filtering.
Empty/error outcome: No record is modified; the user can revise filters or return to the list.

[183] Heading3

[184] table
Primary Actors | Administrator | Secondary Actors | None specified by the UC source

2. The system checks role authorization and presents the permitted scope of records.
3. The actor selects the target Selected account by Account identifier.
4. The system validates referenced record identity, supplied filter values and date-range order where applicable.
5. The system retrieves matching records using their current persisted links and permissions.
6. The system displays Staff name, role, account status, permitted operations with the relevant linked source references and recorded statuses.
7. The actor may inspect a result or adjust filters; this read-only action does not create, update or post inventory.
Alternative Flows | A1 — Step 2: Actor lacks view permission; show MSG-08 and reveal no protected data.
A2 — Step 3/4: A selected record identifier does not exist or is outside authorized scope; show a non-disclosing not-found/permission result.
A3 — Step 3/5: No record matches the filters; show MSG-01 and an empty list rather than sample or fabricated rows.
A4 — Step 4: Filters conflict (for example, From Date after To Date) or have an invalid value; highlight the filter, leave the stored data unchanged.
A5 — Step 5/6: A linked document is unavailable or historical/inactive; preserve the original reference and present only data for which the actor has access.
Postconditions | Successful outcome: The actor has seen the Selected account and its relevant Staff name, role, account status, permitted operations.
Persisted business data: No creation, status transition, inventory change or approval occurs from viewing or filtering.
Empty/error outcome: No record is modified; the user can revise filters or return to the list.

Normal Flow | 1. A administrator opens SC-02 and selects “Assign roles and operation permissions”.

4. System displays available business operations and current role assignments
5. Administrator selects the role and its permitted view/create/update/post actions
6. System validates and saves the permission configuration used by subsequent operations.
7. The system validates all mandatory input fields, cross-document quantities, actor authorization and referenced record state before accepting confirmation.
8. On success the system saves the specific business outcome and updates related documents or eligible stock only if this UC explicitly performs a stock-posting operation.
9. The system displays the resulting record/status and source references; inventory-affecting operations retain the before/after movement details.
Alternative Flows | A1 — At step 2, access/role check fails: show MSG-08 and reveal no protected record; no data are changed.

Postconditions | Successful outcome: Subsequent authorization checks use saved operation-level permissions.

Failure outcome: Missing inputs, a rejected action or a failed validation leave existing posted transactions and inventory unchanged.

2.1 UC-08: View product list

[200] table
Primary Actors | Store Manager, Accountant, Warehouse Staff, Sales Staff | Secondary Actors | None specified by the UC source
Description | As an authorized Store Manager/Accountant/Warehouse Staff/Sales Staff user, I want to view product list so that search products by item code, name, size, and status.
Related feature/screen: FE-02 — Product, Lot and Unit Management / SC-03.
Preconditions | Access: The actor is signed in and authorized to view Product list under UC-08.
Reference data: Product master is readable.
Optional search/filter inputs: Product code, name, nominal size, status. No filters are mandatory; the actor may run an unfiltered list/report within permission scope.
Display fields: Product code, category, size, product status. Values are retrieved from saved records; any displayed quantity is reconciled from the corresponding business transactions.
Result scope: Access rights and existing document/quality states determine which records can be opened.
Normal Flow | 1. The actor navigates to SC-03 and selects “View product list”.
2. The system checks role authorization and presents the permitted scope of records.
3. The actor optionally enters filters: Product code, name, nominal size, status.
4. The system validates referenced record identity, supplied filter values and date-range order where applicable.
5. The system retrieves matching records using their current persisted links and permissions.
6. The system displays Product code, category, size, product status with the relevant linked source references and recorded statuses.
7. The actor may inspect a result or adjust filters; this read-only action does not create, update or post inventory.
Alternative Flows | A1 — Step 2: Actor lacks view permission; show MSG-08 and reveal no protected data.
A2 — Step 3/4: A selected record identifier does not exist or is outside authorized scope; show a non-disclosing not-found/permission result.
A3 — Step 3/5: No record matches the filters; show MSG-01 and an empty list rather than sample or fabricated rows.
A4 — Step 4: Filters conflict (for example, From Date after To Date) or have an invalid value; highlight the filter, leave the stored data unchanged.
A5 — Step 5/6: A linked document is unavailable or historical/inactive; preserve the original reference and present only data for which the actor has access.
Postconditions | Successful outcome: The actor has seen the Product list and its relevant Product code, category, size, product status.
Persisted business data: No creation, status transition, inventory change or approval occurs from viewing or filtering.
Empty/error outcome: No record is modified; the user can revise filters or return to the list.

A4 — Step 4: Filters conflict (for example, From Date after To Date) or have an invalid value; highlight the filter, leave the stored data unchanged.
A5 — Step 5/6: A linked document is unavailable or historical/inactive; preserve the original reference and present only data for which the actor has access.
Postconditions | Successful outcome: The actor has seen the Selected supplier and its relevant Contact fields, related purchases, receipts, returns.
Persisted business data: No creation, status transition, inventory change or approval occurs from viewing or filtering.
Empty/error outcome: No record is modified; the user can revise filters or return to the list.

7.1 UC-47: View stock by lot, location, and quality status

[322] table
Primary Actors | Store Manager, Accountant, Warehouse Staff, Sales Staff | Secondary Actors | None specified by the UC source
Description | As an authorized Store Manager/Accountant/Warehouse Staff/Sales Staff user, I want to view stock by lot, location, and quality status so that filter inventory by product, warehouse, lot, tone, calibre, and quality status.
Related feature/screen: FE-07 — Inventory by Lot, Location and Status / SC-10.
Preconditions | Access: The actor is signed in and authorized to view Inventory balance list under UC-47.
Reference data: Inventory balances exist.
Optional search/filter inputs: Warehouse, product, lot, location, quality status. No filters are mandatory; the actor may run an unfiltered list/report within permission scope.
Display fields: Lot, location, actual/usable qty, status. Values are retrieved from saved records; any displayed quantity is reconciled from the corresponding business transactions.
Result scope: Access rights and existing document/quality states determine which records can be opened.
Normal Flow | 1. The actor navigates to SC-10 and selects “View stock by lot, location, and quality status”.
2. The system checks role authorization and presents the permitted scope of records.
3. The actor optionally enters filters: Warehouse, product, lot, location, quality status.
4. The system validates referenced record identity, supplied filter values and date-range order where applicable.
5. The system retrieves matching records using their current persisted links and permissions.
6. The system displays Lot, location, actual/usable qty, status with the relevant linked source references and recorded statuses.
7. The actor may inspect a result or adjust filters; this read-only action does not create, update or post inventory.
Alternative Flows | A1 — Step 2: Actor lacks view permission; show MSG-08 and reveal no protected data.
A2 — Step 3/4: A selected record identifier does not exist or is outside authorized scope; show a non-disclosing not-found/permission result.
A3 — Step 3/5: No record matches the filters; show MSG-01 and an empty list rather than sample or fabricated rows.
A4 — Step 4: Filters conflict (for example, From Date after To Date) or have an invalid value; highlight the filter, leave the stored data unchanged.
A5 — Step 5/6: A linked document is unavailable or historical/inactive; preserve the original reference and present only data for which the actor has access.
Postconditions | Successful outcome: The actor has seen the Inventory balance list and its relevant Lot, location, actual/usable qty, status.
Persisted business data: No creation, status transition, inventory change or approval occurs from viewing or filtering.
Empty/error outcome: No record is modified; the user can revise filters or return to the list.

9.1 UC-70: View stocktake list

[393] table
Primary Actors | Store Manager, Warehouse Staff | Secondary Actors | None specified by the UC source
Description | As an authorized Store Manager/Warehouse Staff user, I want to view stocktake list so that search stocktakes by warehouse, scope, cutoff time, and status.
Related feature/screen: FE-09 — Stocktake and Inventory Adjustment / SC-14.
Preconditions | Access: The actor is signed in and authorized to view Stocktake list under UC-70.
Reference data: Stocktakes exist.
Optional search/filter inputs: Warehouse, scope, cutoff date/time, status. No filters are mandatory; the actor may run an unfiltered list/report within permission scope.
Display fields: Stocktake reference, scope, cutoff, status. Values are retrieved from saved records; any displayed quantity is reconciled from the corresponding business transactions.
Result scope: Access rights and existing document/quality states determine which records can be opened.
Normal Flow | 1. The actor navigates to SC-14 and selects “View stocktake list”.
2. The system checks role authorization and presents the permitted scope of records.
3. The actor optionally enters filters: Warehouse, scope, cutoff date/time, status.
4. The system validates referenced record identity, supplied filter values and date-range order where applicable.
5. The system retrieves matching records using their current persisted links and permissions.
6. The system displays Stocktake reference, scope, cutoff, status with the relevant linked source references and recorded statuses.
7. The actor may inspect a result or adjust filters; this read-only action does not create, update or post inventory.
Alternative Flows | A1 — Step 2: Actor lacks view permission; show MSG-08 and reveal no protected data.
A2 — Step 3/4: A selected record identifier does not exist or is outside authorized scope; show a non-disclosing not-found/permission result.
A3 — Step 3/5: No record matches the filters; show MSG-01 and an empty list rather than sample or fabricated rows.
A4 — Step 4: Filters conflict (for example, From Date after To Date) or have an invalid value; highlight the filter, leave the stored data unchanged.
A5 — Step 5/6: A linked document is unavailable or historical/inactive; preserve the original reference and present only data for which the actor has access.
Postconditions | Successful outcome: The actor has seen the Stocktake list and its relevant Stocktake reference, scope, cutoff, status.
Persisted business data: No creation, status transition, inventory change or approval occurs from viewing or filtering.
Empty/error outcome: No record is modified; the user can revise filters or return to the list.

[430] table
Primary Actors | Sales Staff, Warehouse Staff, Store Manager | Secondary Actors | None specified by the UC source
Description | As an authorized Sales Staff/Warehouse Staff/Store Manager user, I want to view goods issue request list so that search issues requested against source documents and processing status.
Related feature/screen: FE-10 — Warehouse Issue / SC-16.
Preconditions | Access: The actor is signed in and authorized to view Goods issue request list under UC-82.
Reference data: Issue requests exist.
Optional search/filter inputs: Source document, request date, status. No filters are mandatory; the actor may run an unfiltered list/report within permission scope.
Display fields: Request, source ref, quantities requested/issued. Values are retrieved from saved records; any displayed quantity is reconciled from the corresponding business transactions.
Result scope: Access rights and existing document/quality states determine which records can be opened.
Normal Flow | 1. The actor navigates to SC-16 and selects “View goods issue request list”.
2. The system checks role authorization and presents the permitted scope of records.
3. The actor optionally enters filters: Source document, request date, status.
4. The system validates referenced record identity, supplied filter values and date-range order where applicable.
5. The system retrieves matching records using their current persisted links and permissions.
6. The system displays Request, source ref, quantities requested/issued with the relevant linked source references and recorded statuses.
7. The actor may inspect a result or adjust filters; this read-only action does not create, update or post inventory.
Alternative Flows | A1 — Step 2: Actor lacks view permission; show MSG-08 and reveal no protected data.
A2 — Step 3/4: A selected record identifier does not exist or is outside authorized scope; show a non-disclosing not-found/permission result.
A3 — Step 3/5: No record matches the filters; show MSG-01 and an empty list rather than sample or fabricated rows.
A4 — Step 4: Filters conflict (for example, From Date after To Date) or have an invalid value; highlight the filter, leave the stored data unchanged.
A5 — Step 5/6: A linked document is unavailable or historical/inactive; preserve the original reference and present only data for which the actor has access.
Postconditions | Successful outcome: The actor has seen the Goods issue request list and its relevant Request, source ref, quantities requested/issued.
Persisted business data: No creation, status transition, inventory change or approval occurs from viewing or filtering.
Empty/error outcome: No record is modified; the user can revise filters or return to the list.

Description | As an authorized Store Manager/Warehouse Staff user, I want to view warehouse transfer report so that track dispatched, in-transit, received, and discrepancy quantities.
Related feature/screen: FE-12 — Warehouse Reports / SC-20.
Preconditions | Access: The actor is signed in and authorized to view Warehouse transfer report under UC-105.
Reference data: Transfer records exist.
Optional search/filter inputs: Date range, source/destination warehouse. No filters are mandatory; the actor may run an unfiltered list/report within permission scope.
Display fields: Dispatched, in transit, received, discrepancy. Values are retrieved from saved records; any displayed quantity is reconciled from the corresponding business transactions.
Result scope: Access rights and existing document/quality states determine which records can be opened.
Normal Flow | 1. The actor navigates to SC-20 and selects “View warehouse transfer report”.
2. The system checks role authorization and presents the permitted scope of records.
3. The actor optionally enters filters: Date range, source/destination warehouse.
4. The system validates referenced record identity, supplied filter values and date-range order where applicable.
5. The system retrieves matching records using their current persisted links and permissions.
6. The system displays Dispatched, in transit, received, discrepancy with the relevant linked source references and recorded statuses.
7. The actor may inspect a result or adjust filters; this read-only action does not create, update or post inventory.
Alternative Flows | A1 — Step 2: Actor lacks view permission; show MSG-08 and reveal no protected data.
A2 — Step 3/4: A selected record identifier does not exist or is outside authorized scope; show a non-disclosing not-found/permission result.
A3 — Step 3/5: No record matches the filters; show MSG-01 and an empty list rather than sample or fabricated rows.
A4 — Step 4: Filters conflict (for example, From Date after To Date) or have an invalid value; highlight the filter, leave the stored data unchanged.
A5 — Step 5/6: A linked document is unavailable or historical/inactive; preserve the original reference and present only data for which the actor has access.
Postconditions | Successful outcome: The actor has seen the Warehouse transfer report and its relevant Dispatched, in transit, received, discrepancy.
Persisted business data: No creation, status transition, inventory change or approval occurs from viewing or filtering.
Empty/error outcome: No record is modified; the user can revise filters or return to the list.

[516] Heading1
III. Functional Requirements

[517] 
Every list mockup displays illustrative records in its on-screen results table. Mockup data do not represent customer operational records. The Field Name / Description table below each screen specifies recommended Microsoft SQL Server types, lengths/precision, entry requirements, defaults and validations. A required field must be populated in the stated workflow step; an Optional filter does not persist to a transaction; a Read-only field is populated from existing records or calculations. Technical data type recommendations do not introduce unapproved workflows or approval rules.

[518] Heading2
1. Access and Role Management (FE-01)

[519] Heading3
1.1 Sign In

[520] Heading4
1.1.1 SC-01: Sign In

[522] 
Mockup SC-01. Sign In — illustrative functional layout

[523] 
Purpose: Access authorized functions according to the assigned account and role.

[524] 
Mapped Use Cases: UC-01 (Sign in to the system)

[525] 
This screen allows only the relevant authorized roles to perform the mapped actions. Each write operation checks current document state, item/lot/location scope and approval/eligible-stock rules.

[526] table
UC | Authorized Actor(s) | Functional Behavior / Expected Outcome
UC-01 | Store Manager, Accountant, Warehouse Staff, Sales Staff, Administrator | Access authorized functions according to the assigned account and role.

[528] 
Field Description

[529] table
Field Name | Description
Username | NVARCHAR(50). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Account login identifier; trim surrounding whitespace and compare against existing usernames without creating duplicates.
Password | NVARCHAR(128) (input only; hash persisted). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Required masked credential; verify against a salted one-way hash. Never store plaintext or display it in activity logs.

[531] Heading3
1.2 Account List and Details

[532] Heading4
1.2.1 SC-02: Account List and Details

[534] 
Mockup SC-02. Account List and Details — illustrative functional layout

[535] 
Purpose: Search and filter accounts by name, role, and active status. Display the selected account's information, assigned role, and status. Register an internal user account and select an appropriate role. Edit allowed account information without losing its action history. Enable or prevent account sign-in while preserving historical documents. Configure view, create, edit, and transaction-recording permissions by role.

[536] 
Mapped Use Cases: UC-02 (View account list), UC-03 (View account details), UC-04 (Create staff account), UC-05 (Update staff account), UC-06 (Activate or deactivate account), UC-07 (Assign roles and operation permissions)

[537] 
This screen allows only the relevant authorized roles to perform the mapped actions. Each write operation checks current document state, item/lot/location scope and approval/eligible-stock rules.

[538] table
UC | Authorized Actor(s) | Functional Behavior / Expected Outcome
UC-02 | Administrator | Search and filter accounts by name, role, and active status.
UC-03 | Administrator | Display the selected account's information, assigned role, and status.
UC-04 | Administrator | Register an internal user account and select an appropriate role.
UC-05 | Administrator | Edit allowed account information without losing its action history.
UC-06 | Administrator | Enable or prevent account sign-in while preserving historical documents.
UC-07 | Administrator | Configure view, create, edit, and transaction-recording permissions by role.

[540] 
Field Description

[541] table
Field Name | Description
Search | NVARCHAR(150) (filter only). Entry: Optional filter. Default: NULL (blank filter is ignored).Validation: Optional keyword matched to the authorized list; no value is saved to a business record.
Role | BIGINT (reference ID). Entry: Required on account create / optional filter. Default: NULL (blank filter is ignored).Validation: Choose an existing authorized internal role; store its reference ID, not free text.
Account Status | BIT (0=inactive; 1=active). Entry: System-controlled. Default: Assigned by the workflow (no fixed SQL DEFAULT).Validation: System-maintained activation flag; inactive accounts cannot authenticate and existing audit references remain.
Username | NVARCHAR(50). Entry: Required on account create. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Account login identifier; trim surrounding whitespace and compare against existing usernames without creating duplicates.
Permissions | BIT per permission (role-operation junction). Entry: Required when assigning role rights. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Store the permitted role-to-operation flags; only the administrator may modify operation assignments.

[543] Heading2
2. Product, Lot and Unit Management (FE-02)

[544] Heading3
2.1 Product and Unit Conversion

[545] Heading4
2.1.1 SC-03: Product and Unit Conversion

[547] 
Mockup SC-03. Product and Unit Conversion — illustrative functional layout

[548] 
Purpose: Search products by item code, name, size, and status. Display tile attributes, packaging specifications, and units of measure. Record product code, category, brand, material, nominal size, and identifying characteristics. Edit allowed attributes without corrupting existing documents or inventory history. Prevent selection on new documents while retaining inventory and history. Define pieces per box and area per piece for box, piece, and square-meter conversion by product.

[549] 
Mapped Use Cases: UC-08 (View product list), UC-09 (View product details), UC-10 (Create product), UC-11 (Update product), UC-12 (Activate or deactivate product), UC-13 (Configure packaging and unit conversion)

[550] 
This screen allows only the relevant authorized roles to perform the mapped actions. Each write operation checks current document state, item/lot/location scope and approval/eligible-stock rules.

[551] table
UC | Authorized Actor(s) | Functional Behavior / Expected Outcome
UC-08 | Store Manager, Accountant, Warehouse Staff, Sales Staff | Search products by item code, name, size, and status.
UC-09 | Store Manager, Accountant, Warehouse Staff, Sales Staff | Display tile attributes, packaging specifications, and units of measure.
UC-10 | Store Manager | Record product code, category, brand, material, nominal size, and identifying characteristics.
UC-11 | Store Manager | Edit allowed attributes without corrupting existing documents or inventory history.
UC-12 | Store Manager | Prevent selection on new documents while retaining inventory and history.
UC-13 | Store Manager | Define pieces per box and area per piece for box, piece, and square-meter conversion by product.

[553] 
Field Description

[554] table
Field Name | Description
Product Code | NVARCHAR(50). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Business product identifier; required on product creation and unique across the product catalog.
Product Name | NVARCHAR(150). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Descriptive tile name; required on product creation; trim whitespace and reject an empty value.
Tile Category | NVARCHAR(100). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Select the tile functional category from the configured reference list.
Brand | NVARCHAR(100). Entry: Optional. Default: NULL.Validation: Optional brand name/reference associated with the product.
Material | NVARCHAR(100). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Select the material classification for the tile product.
Nominal Size | NVARCHAR(100). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Nominal tile dimension label, such as 600×600 mm; preserve it separately from lot actual calibre.
Pieces / Box | INT. Entry: Required when configuring conversion. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Whole pieces per package; must be greater than zero when configuring conversion.
m² / Piece | DECIMAL(18,6). Entry: Required when configuring conversion. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Square-meter conversion factor per piece; must be greater than zero and used consistently in stock movements.
Status | VARCHAR(30). Entry: System-controlled. Default: Assigned by the workflow (no fixed SQL DEFAULT).Validation: Workflow-dependent state; display the current status and accept only transitions authorized by the relevant UC.

[556] 
Input and validation notes: SQL Server types and maximum lengths are the proposed implementation baseline. Reference inputs must resolve to existing eligible master/source records; quantities must respect the product unit conversion and current lot/location quality state. Read-only balances, audit details and approval actor/timestamps are system-populated. Failed validation must not partially post any inventory movement.

Field Description

[567] table
Field Name | Description
Product | BIGINT (reference ID). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Existing product reference; selection must respect product activation and the current workflow.

Production Date | DATE. Entry: Optional. Default: NULL.Validation: Optional actual manufacturing date; reject invalid calendar dates and never substitute a guessed date.
Transaction History | Derived result set (no standalone SQL column). Entry: Read-only. Default: Derived from persisted data; not user-entered.Validation: Read-only list of posted documents and movements relating to the selected lot; generated from audit records.

[592] 
Field Description

[593] table
Field Name | Description
Request No. | NVARCHAR(50). Entry: System-generated. Default: Assigned by the workflow (no fixed SQL DEFAULT).Validation: System-assigned replenishment request code; unique among replenishment documents and read-only after creation.
Request Date | DATE. Entry: System-generated. Default: Assigned by the workflow (no fixed SQL DEFAULT).Validation: Creation date of the request; system-populated and read-only.
Requested By | BIGINT (reference ID). Entry: System-generated. Default: Assigned by the workflow (no fixed SQL DEFAULT).Validation: Reference to the authenticated employee/account that submitted the document; system-populated.
Reference Stock | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Read-only. Default: Derived from persisted data; not user-entered.Validation: Read-only on-hand quantity for the relevant product/warehouse at request creation; never treated as an editable stock movement.
Product | BIGINT (reference ID). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Existing product reference; selection must respect product activation and the current workflow.
Requested Quantity | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Quantity requested on a replenishment or issue line; greater than zero on submission.
Reason | NVARCHAR(500). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Business justification; required on requests and variance/return actions that explicitly require a reason.
Approval Decision | VARCHAR(30). Entry: Required when deciding. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Approve or reject an eligible pending request; capture approver and decision time; rejection requires a reason.

[600]

[602] table
UC | Authorized Actor(s) | Functional Behavior / Expected Outcome

UC-28 | Store Manager, Accountant, Warehouse Staff | Display order lines, supplier confirmations, and partial receipt history.
UC-29 | Accountant | Create an order from an approved request, specifying supplier, products, quantities, units, and expected receipt date.
UC-30 | Accountant | Change only the unreceived portion without deleting quantities already received.
UC-31 | Accountant | Record confirmed quantities and anticipated delivery schedule, including partial confirmation.
UC-32 | Store Manager, Accountant, Warehouse Staff | Compare ordered, supplier-confirmed, delivered/received, and outstanding quantities by order line.

[604] 
Field Description

[605] table
Field Name | Description
Order No. | NVARCHAR(50). Entry: System-generated. Default: Assigned by the workflow (no fixed SQL DEFAULT).Validation: Unique purchase order document number, generated or assigned at PO creation and displayed read-only thereafter.
Supplier | BIGINT (reference ID). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Existing supplier reference; a new purchase order may use only an active supplier.
Product | BIGINT (reference ID). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Existing product reference; selection must respect product activation and the current workflow.
Ordered Quantity | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Positive purchase order line quantity; not lower than quantity already received when edited.
Unit | VARCHAR(10). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Selected product unit of measure: piece, box, or m²; use the product-specific conversion factor.
Expected Receipt | DATE. Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Expected goods arrival date recorded on a purchase order; calendar date only.
Confirmed Quantity | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Required at confirmation. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Supplier-confirmed line quantity; nonnegative and reconciled against the applicable ordered quantity.
Received Quantity | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Read-only. Default: Derived from persisted data; not user-entered.Validation: Actual quantity captured at a receiving checkpoint; nonnegative and linked to the source order.
Outstanding Quantity | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Read-only. Default: Derived from persisted data; not user-entered.Validation: Read-only remaining purchase quantity calculated from ordered and received quantities with relevant confirmation state.

Field Description

[618] table
Field Name | Description
Receipt No. | NVARCHAR(50). Entry: System-generated. Default: Assigned by the workflow (no fixed SQL DEFAULT).Validation: Unique goods receipt document code; associates inspected goods with the source purchase order.
Purchase Order | BIGINT (reference ID). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Source purchase order reference; must exist and remain linked to the receipt.
Delivery Reference | NVARCHAR(50). Entry: Required for external delivery identification. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: External delivery note/reference for the shipment; check repeated reference against the relevant supplier and PO.
Product | BIGINT (reference ID). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Existing product reference; selection must respect product activation and the current workflow.
Lot | BIGINT (reference ID). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Existing lot record reference; lot/tone/calibre must match the selected product.
Tone | NVARCHAR(100). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Actual lot shade/tone; required when registering a lot and used to distinguish physical stock.
Actual Calibre | NVARCHAR(100). Entry: Required. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Actual lot dimension/calibre value, independent of the product nominal size.
Boxes | INT. Entry: Conditional: quantity may be entered in boxes/pieces/m². Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Nonnegative integer number of cartons/boxes physically received; converted using pieces per box.
Pieces | INT. Entry: Conditional: quantity may be entered in boxes/pieces/m². Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Nonnegative integer quantity of individual pieces physically received; do not use floating-point units.
m² | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Conditional: quantity may be entered in boxes/pieces/m². Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Nonnegative measured square-meter quantity converted by the selected product conversion factors.
Accepted | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Required at inspection. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Accepted goods quantity at inspection; must be nonnegative and included in the total received reconciliation.
Pending Inspection | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Required at inspection. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Quantity awaiting inspection; cannot count toward usable stock.
Defective | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Required at inspection. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Quantity determined defective during receipt inspection; cannot be included as accepted/usable stock.
Rejected | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Required at inspection. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Quantity rejected on arrival; remain segregated from accepted inventory.
Location | BIGINT (reference ID). Entry: Required when posting accepted stock. Default: No SQL DEFAULT; supply or select a value.Validation: Existing location reference within the chosen warehouse; must be active/eligible for a new stock posting.

Variance | DECIMAL(18,3). Entry: Read-only calculated. Default: Derived from persisted data; not user-entered.Validation: Signed difference between a physical amount and its comparison baseline; can be positive, zero or negative.

[794] Heading1
IV. Non-Functional Requirements

[795] Heading2
1. External Interfaces

[796] table
Interface | Requirement | Acceptance / Verification
Web user interface | Provide feature-specific forms, lists, filters and confirmation actions with role-appropriate access; clearly distinguish product, lot, tone, calibre, location and quality state. | Review SC-01 through SC-21 and ensure relevant fields are visible without confusing quality or quantity types.
Document and evidence links | Defect, discrepancy, inventory adjustment and return records must retain source document and supporting evidence associations. | Authorized staff can trace the record to its originating transaction and supporting documents.
External integration | No technical third-party interface is established by the current UCs. | New integrations require source documents, specified interfaces and a change request before implementation.
Printing / export | The UC list identifies report viewing but does not specify print forms, export formats or electronic signatures. | Do not treat unsupported export/printing as a signed-off system requirement.

[798] Heading2
2. Quality Attributes

[799] Heading3
2.1 Usability

[800] table
Requirement | Specification | Verification
Screen consistency | Lists consistently show search, filters and results; details consistently display document references and transaction history. | Compare interactions across SC-02–SC-21.
Quality status clarity | Defective/pending goods must not appear as usable stock; ordered, confirmed, dispatched, handed-over and received quantities must have distinct headings. | Test UC-32, UC-47, UC-62 and UC-67.
Validation feedback | Unauthorized, invalid-state and excess-stock operations show a clear actionable message without committing a partial change. | Exercise the alternative paths in Part II and system messages in Appendix.
Data entry accuracy | Choose active master data rather than free-entering unrelated codes; display conversion assumptions for each product. | Test UC-13, UC-36, UC-74 and UC-88.

[802] Heading3
2.2 Performance

[803] 
No source file provides a signed-off response-time SLA, simultaneous-user count, maximum inventory volume or availability percentage. Accordingly, this version specifies verifiable behavior while leaving numeric limits open for customer confirmation.

[804] table
Requirement | Target / Behavior | Condition / Verification
List and filter behavior | Return correctly scoped results, report empty matches and prevent duplicate posting from repeated actions. | Test on representative customer-approved data sets when available.
Transaction consistency | Goods receipt, movement, issue, return and adjustment operations must not produce partially committed balances. | Reconcile UC-40,60,64,66,81,89,101 with movement records.
Report reconciliation | Closing quantity equals opening quantity plus applicable inbound, outbound and adjustment movements within the same reporting scope. | Cross-check UC-102 against stock movements UC-107.
Capacity and SLA | Numerical targets are not specified in supplied requirements. | Confirm measurable values during acceptance criteria review.

[806] Heading3
2.3 Security

[807] table
Requirement | Description | Verification
Authentication | Only active authenticated accounts can access internal operations. | Test UC-01, UC-06 and UC-07.
Role-based authorization | Each data read and state change checks the assigned role/UC permission, not merely whether a UI button is visible. | Attempt direct requests for actions marked No in the permission matrix.
Credential protection | Credentials are not stored or logged in directly readable form. | Check stored data and event logs for plaintext passwords.
Audit integrity | Every change to stock records source document, operator, timestamp and original/resulting quantity. | Trace UC-107–109 back to affected documents.
Evidence access | Only roles assigned to the relevant business record may access discrepancy/defect evidence. | Test UC-38,39,55,67,68,98 under allowed and denied roles.
Approval protection | Pending or rejected proposals cannot trigger an unauthorized quantity adjustment. | Test UC-49,55,79–81,95,100.

[809] Heading3
2.4 Reliability and Data Integrity

[810] 
Posting of accepted receipts, intra-warehouse movement, transfer dispatch/receipt, approved adjustment, actual issue and supplier return must be atomic at the document/line level. Repeated confirmation or interrupted operations must not increase or decrease the same eligible quantity more than once. This describes the required business outcome; it does not prescribe a particular database technology.

[811] Heading3
2.5 Maintainability and Traceability

[812] 
Each requirement retains its FE/UC identifier and is mapped to a screen and a fully specified use case. Data-rule changes must be assessed for impacts on unit conversion, source document linkage, approval steps and movement history. A revision must never silently rewrite the meaning of an existing UC ID.

[813] Heading1
V. Requirement Appendix

[814] Heading2
1. Business Rules

[815] table
GBR-ID | Rule Statement | Applies To | Rationale | Enforced By
GB-01 | Only a role explicitly authorized for a UC may read or perform its corresponding operation. | All UCs | Segregates responsibilities and protects transactions. | Enforce permissions on every request.
GB-02 | Inactive accounts, products, suppliers and locations cannot be used in new operations; history is preserved. | UC-06,12,22,45 | Historical transactions remain explainable. | Validate master-record state when creating documents.
GB-03 | Convert boxes/pieces/square meters using the selected product configuration. | UC-13,36,74,89 | Prevents conversion errors across tile products. | Use and verify the product-specific conversion factors.
GB-04 | Only inspected accepted quantities may increase usable inventory. | UC-37–40,49–50,92–95 | Prevents issue of pending or defective goods. | Reconcile accepted quality quantities before posting.
GB-05 | Partially received purchase orders can change only quantities not yet received. | UC-30–32 | Preserves source receipt history. | Check already posted receipt quantity.
GB-06 | Intra-warehouse location movement does not change total warehouse balance. | UC-59–60 | Preserves inventory totals. | Post equal and opposite changes across locations.
GB-07 | Warehouse transfers distinguish dispatch, handover and receipt; variances require approved resolution. | UC-63–69 | Keeps in-transit stock accountable. | Reconcile three checkpoints and evidence.
GB-08 | Stocktake uses an immutable balance snapshot and records subsequent movements separately. | UC-72–76 | Prevents incorrect variance calculations. | Retain stocktake cut-off timestamp and original values.
GB-09 | An inventory adjustment requires approval and can be posted at most once. | UC-79–81 | Prevents unauthorized or duplicate stock changes. | Check approval and posted flag on each attempt.
GB-10 | Goods issue deducts only actual issued stock by lot and location. | UC-87–89 | Prevents incorrect or negative inventory. | Validate usable quantity before posting.
GB-11 | Customer returns cannot increase usable stock before approved disposition. | UC-92–95 | Protects goods quality control. | Check inspection and decision state.
GB-12 | Goods rejected at original receipt cannot be deducted again when returned to supplier. | UC-37,40,101 | Prevents double deduction. | Reconcile original receipt inventory impact.
GB-13 | Every stock movement must link source document, user, time and before/after quantities. | UC-107–109 | Enables audit and lot traceability. | Store immutable posting metadata.

[817] Heading2
2. System Messages

[818] table
# | Message Code | Type | Context | Content
1 | MSG-01 | Inline | No matching search results | No matching records were found.
2 | MSG-02 | Field error | A mandatory field is empty | Please enter {field_name}.
3 | MSG-03 | Field error | Quantity has an invalid value | {field_name} must be a valid non-negative amount.
4 | MSG-04 | Field error | Quantity exceeds usable stock | Requested quantity exceeds usable stock for the selected lot and location.
5 | MSG-05 | Inline | Selected reference is inactive | {entity_name} is inactive and cannot be selected on a new document.
6 | MSG-06 | Confirmation | User confirms inventory posting | Confirm the actual quantity to be posted for this document?
7 | MSG-07 | Toast | Document is saved | {document_type} has been saved.
8 | MSG-08 | Inline | Access is not authorized | You do not have permission to perform this action.
9 | MSG-09 | Inline | Required approval is missing | The document cannot be processed before the required approval.
10 | MSG-10 | Field error | Rejection / discrepancy reason is missing | Please provide a reason for rejecting or resolving the discrepancy.
11 | MSG-11 | Inline | Duplicate lot identity | A lot with these identifying attributes already exists.
12 | MSG-12 | Inline | Location still contains stock | The location cannot be deactivated while stock remains.
13 | MSG-13 | Inline | Posting has already been completed | This document has already affected stock; duplicate posting is blocked.
14 | MSG-14 | Inline | Inspection classification does not reconcile | Classification totals must equal the actual received quantity.
15 | MSG-15 | Inline | Authentication failed | Sign-in credentials are invalid.
16 | MSG-16 | Toast | Stocktake result saved | Count results and associated variances have been saved.
17 | MSG-17 | Inline | Transfer quantities differ | Dispatch, handover and receipt values do not match; record a discrepancy.
18 | MSG-18 | Toast | Approval request rejected | The rejection decision and its reason have been recorded.
19 | MSG-19 | Inline | Purchase order partly received | The received portion of a purchase order cannot be canceled or reduced.
20 | MSG-20 | Inline | Return has not been approved | Goods cannot be restocked or returned to the supplier before disposition approval.

[820] Heading2
3. Other Requirements
